### ...of the Ancient Sages.

Yet note this very well, my friend, that the **Bridegroom** must marry his **Bride** naked and bare; for this reason, all the prepared things for the adornment of their clothes and the necessary decoration of their faces must be taken from them again, so that they occupy the grave entirely bare, just as they were born bare, so that their seed may not be destroyed by foreign intermingling. <note>In alchemy, "nakedness" refers to substances being stripped of all impurities or "accidental" qualities before they are combined in the vessel (the "grave").</note>

In conclusion of this <term>discourse</term> <note>original: "Sermons" - often used in hermetic literature to denote a specific lesson or speech</note>, I tell you in all truth that the most precious water, in which the **Bridegroom** is to have his bath, must be made very wisely and with great care from two **fighters** (understand: from two <gloss>Sulfur and Mercury <note>original: "Sulphur et Mercurius"</note></gloss> opposing materials). 
<margin>⸮ NB</margin> For one combatant must drive the other forth, so that they are sent to the struggle and can attain knighthood. For it is of no use to the **Eagle** <note>The Eagle often symbolizes the volatile (evaporating) component of the work</note> to make his nest alone in the high mountains, for his young must freeze because of the snow that has settled on the peaks of the mountains.

# Of the Great Stone

Through water, fire can be destroyed and entirely extinguished; and if one pours much water into a little fire, the fire must be obedient to the water and grant it the dominion of victory. In the same way, our

<margin>e</margin> prevail once again. Yet no triumph can occur in such

<margin>c</margin> simple nature and a grand

### ...of the Ancient Sages.

All flesh born from the Earth must be destroyed and return to Earth, just as it was Earth before. At that point, the earthly salt <note>original: *Saltz* — in alchemy, Salt represents the "fixed" or physical principle that remains after fire has driven off volatile liquids and gases.</note> grants a new birth through a heavenly awakening. For where no earth is first made, no resurrection <note>A reference to the "resurrection" of the alchemical material into a more perfect, purified state.</note> can follow in our Work; because within the earth lies the natural balsam <note>A life-preserving essence or "healing spirit" believed to be hidden within the heart of matter.</note> and the salt of those who have sought the knowledge of all things.

At the final Judgment of the world, the world shall be judged by fire. That which was previously created from nothing by the Master must once again become ashes through fire. From those same ashes, the Phoenix <note>The mythical bird that burns and rises from its own ashes, symbolizing the successful completion of a stage where the matter is purified and reborn.</note> will finally bring forth its young again. For in such ashes is truly found the right Tartarus <note>original: *Tartarus* — while literally referring to the stony sediment in wine casks, in this context it refers to the essential mineral crust or "hellish" residue that must be purified.</note>, which must be dissolved, and after its dissolution <note>original: *solution*</note>, the firm lock of the Royal Chamber <note>The "Royal Chamber" is a metaphor for the inner secret or the perfected state of the Philosopher's Stone.</note> can be opened.

A new heaven and a new earth will be made after the burning, and the new man will appear far more glorious than he was in the first world, for he has been transfigured <note>original: *verkleret* — meaning clarified, glorified, or made radiant through the process of purification.</note>.

# On the Great Stone

When ashes and sand are well-ripened <note>original: *gezeitiget* — an alchemical term meaning matured or brought to completion by heat.</note> and thoroughly cooked by the fire, the Master <note>The "Master" or Adept is the alchemist who understands the natural laws of transformation.</note> makes from them a glass that thereafter endures forever in the fire; in color, it resembles a transparent stone, and is no longer recognized as ashes. This is a great secret art to the ignorant, but not to the knowing, for to them it has become a craft through knowledge and frequent experience.

From stones, the Master also burns lime <note>original: *Kalck* — refers to quicklime (calcium oxide), produced by heating limestone. It was a key substance in early chemistry because of the intense heat it generates when touched by water.</note>, so that it can be used for work; and before it is prepared for this by the fire, it is a stone, and cannot be used for the work as lime. But through the fire, the stone is ripened, and takes on a very heated degree <note>The alchemist believes the "spirit" of the fire literally enters the stone and stays there.</note> from the fire, and strengthens itself, and thus becomes so powerful that almost nothing can be compared to the fiery spirit of the lime when it is brought to its perfection.

<margin>//</margin> Every single thing, when it is burned to ashes,
<margin>//</margin> yields its Salt <note>original: *Saltz* — the physical body or "fixed" principle of a substance.</note> through the art. If you can, in its anatomy <note>original: *Anatomia* — the chemical process of separating a substance into its fundamental parts.</note>, specially preserve the Sulfur <note>original: *Schwebel* — the "soul" or oily, combustible principle.</note> and its Mercury <note>original: *Mercurium* — the "spirit" or volatile principle.</note>...

<summary>The author uses the physical transformations of glassmaking and lime-burning to explain how fire purifies and empowers matter, transitioning from theological metaphors to chemical analogies. He introduces the "Tria Prima"—Salt, Sulfur, and Mercury—as the essential components to be extracted through the "anatomy" of a substance.</summary>

# Of the Ancient Sages

...and with the **Salt**, restore it once more, sufficiently according to the **Art** <note>The "Art" refers to the Great Work of Alchemy.</note>, then that which it was before its destruction and its **Anatomy** <note>original: *Anatomia* — in this context, it refers to the alchemical process of "dissecting" or breaking a substance down into its primary components.</note> can be made from it again through the **fire**. The "wise" of this world call this a folly and consider it pure lies; they call it a "new creation" which God has not permitted to a sinner. Yet they themselves do not understand that the creature existed beforehand, and the **Master** only demonstrates its multiplication and mastery through the **seed of nature**.

Whichever **Master** has no **ashes** can also make no **Salt** for our Art; for without Salt, our Work cannot be given a **physical body**, because the preservation of all things is wrought by the bare Salt alone.

Just as **Salt** is a preserver of all things and guards against rot, so too is the **Salt** of our Masters a protection for the **metals**, so that they cannot be entirely annihilated and corrupted to the point that nothing could be made of them again—unless their **Balsam** <note>original: *Balſam* — an alchemical term for the internal, life-preserving moisture or "radical humidity" that keeps a body from decaying.</note> and innate **Salt-spirit** were to die away naturally. For if the body...

# Of the Great Stone

When **ashes** and **sand** are well-ripened and thoroughly cooked by the **fire** / the **Master** makes a **glass** therefrom / that thereafter always withstands the fire / and in color is like a transparent **stone** / and is no longer recognized as mere ashes / that is a great and secret **Art** <note>Refers to the "Great Work" of alchemy.</note> to the ignorant / but not to the knowing / for through knowledge and frequent experience it has become a **craft** to him.

From stones the Master also burns **lime** <note>original: *Kalck* — quicklime, produced by heating limestone.</note> / so that one may use it for work / and before it is prepared by the **fire** / it is a stone / and cannot be used as lime for the work / But through the **fire** the stone is matured / and takes on a very hot degree from the fire / and strengthens itself / and thus becomes so powerful / that almost nothing can be compared to the **fiery spirit** of the **lime** / once it is brought to its perfection.

<margin>//</margin>
Every thing / when it is burned to **ashes** / yields its **Salt** through the **Art** / if you can especially preserve the **Sulfur** <note>original: *Schwebel* — In alchemy, Sulfur represents the "soul" or the flammable, oily principle of a substance.</note> and its **Mercury** <note>original: *Mercurium* — In alchemy, Mercury represents the "spirit" or the volatile, metallic principle.</note> in its **Anatomy** <note>The alchemical process of "dissecting" or separating a substance into its primary components: Salt, Sulfur, and Mercury.</note> /

<summary>The author uses the physical transformations of glassmaking and lime-burning to explain how alchemical fire matures substances, eventually focusing on how every burned thing yields its essential Salt, Sulfur, and Mercury.</summary>

# Of the Great Stone

Life within the Earth brings forth everything that grows from it; and whoever says that the Earth is dead speaks no truth, for a dead thing can impart nothing to a living one, and growth <note>original: "Zugang" — in this context, it refers to the process of increase, accretion, or vital growth.</note> has an end among the dead, for the spirit of life has fled. Therefore, the **Spirit** is the life and soul of the Earth, which dwells within it and receives its influence from the **Heavenly** and **Siderial** <note>Relating to the stars and constellations (*sidus*), which alchemists believed "seeded" the earth with spiritual forces.</note> into the **Terrestrial** <note>The physical, earthly realm.</note>. For all herbs, trees, and roots, as well as all metals and minerals, receive their power, growth, and nourishment from the **Spirit of the Earth**.

# Of the Great Stone

A man without a wife is but half a body, and a wife without a man is likewise to be considered only half a body; for alone they can produce no fruit <term>fruit: the alchemical "tincture" or the Philosopher's Stone itself</term>. But when they live together in marital union <term>marital union: the chemical "coniunctio" or the joining of the solar/masculine and lunar/feminine principles</term>, then the body is complete, and through their seed <term>seed: the generative essence of the metals</term>, a multiplication may follow.

If one casts too much seed upon the field <term>field: the "philosophical earth" or the base matter in the vessel</term>, the soil is overburdened, so that no perfect fruit can result. If, however, there is too little seed, the crop grows thin, and weeds <term>weeds: impurities or "leprosies" of the metal that arise when the proportions are incorrect</term> spring up in its place, from which likewise no benefit can be gained.

Whoever wishes to keep his conscience free from sin in the selling of goods must treat his fellow man with equal measure, providing him with the correct yardstick <term>yardstick: the "ell" (Elle), a traditional measurement of length</term> and weights, so that he may escape the curse and prepare a thank-offering for the poor. In great waters, one is easily drowned; and small waters are easily dried up by the heat of the sun, rendering them of no use. <note>This paragraph serves as a dual instruction: it is both a moral exhortation and a technical warning that too much moisture ("great waters") or too much heat ("heat of the sun") will destroy the alchemical work.</note>

<summary>This page employs allegories of marriage, farming, and honest commerce to emphasize the necessity of "proportion" (Pondera) in alchemy. It explains that the "Great Stone" requires a perfect balance of masculine and feminine principles, as well as precise quantities of matter and heat.</summary>

# Of the Great Stone
->82<-

The fiery King <term>fiery King: a symbol for the sulfurous, masculine principle or the final red stage of the Work</term> will greatly love the lovely voice of the Queen <term>Queen: the mercurial, feminine principle</term> / and because of great love, will kindly take her to himself / and satisfy himself on her / until they both disappear / and enter into one body <note>This "disappearing into one body" describes the *coniunctio*, or chemical marriage, where two substances merge into a single new compound.</note>.

It is said / that two can suppress and overcome the third / especially when they have room / to pour out their malice / Thereupon you should know from a true foundation / that a doubled wind must come / called Vulturnus <note>original: "Vulturnus," the Roman name for the Southeast wind; in alchemy, winds often represent the movement of gases or "spirits" within the vessel</note> / and after that a simple wind / called Norus <note>likely a variation of "Notus," the South wind</note>, these will roar greatly from the East <note>original: "Orient"</note> and the South <note>original: "Mittage"</note> / and move themselves / and when they are all robbed / so that their movement is gone / and out of air, water has become / then you may boldly trust / that out of a spiritual thing a bodily thing will become <note>This refers to "fixation," the process of turning a volatile gas (spirit) into a solid, stable substance (body).</note> / and that the number through the four seasons of the year / in the fourth heaven / after the seven planets <note>The seven metals of antiquity were associated with the seven planets (Sun/Gold, Moon/Silver, etc.).</note> have completed their dominion / shall dominate / and in the lowest dwelling of the palace complete its course / and pass the highest examination / thus then the two who went out have suppressed and consumed the third.

Natural warmth sustains man in life; for once the natural heat has departed, then life comes to an end.

Natural fire, when used moderately, is a protection against the cold; however, an excess of the same brings destruction. It is not necessary that the Sun touch the earth corporally <note>original: "Corporalisch," meaning physically or in its bodily form</note> or physically with its entire substance; rather, it is sufficient that it does its work from afar through its rays <note>original: *radios*</note> and beams. These are strengthened through reflection <note>original: *reflexion*</note> when they fall upon the earth, so that by such means the Sun has sufficient efficacy <note>original: *efficatiam*</note> to perform its office and to ripen everything through its cooking <term>Cooking: Also known as decoction; the application of steady heat to mature the "Stone" or substance, mirroring the natural ripening of fruit by the sun.</term>. For through the distance of the air, the rays of the Sun are brought into a temperate balance <note>original: *temperament*</note>, so that by means of the air, the fire can work—with the help of the air—and the air can work with the help of the fire.

Earth can bring forth nothing without water, and water alone can awaken nothing without the earth. Just as earth and water cannot do without each other—

### ...of the ancient Sages.

All flesh—whether it be human or from animals—can produce no further multiplication and <term>propagation</term> <note>The process of breeding or spreading a species.</note> unless it first occurs through <term>putrefaction</term> <note>The process of decay or rotting. In early science and alchemy, putrefaction was seen as a necessary "death" that allowed new life to be "reborn."</note>. Likewise, the seed of the field and everything that is subject to and part of the <term>vegetative kingdom</term> <note>Plants and plant life.</note> cannot achieve any increase except through putrefaction. Furthermore, many animals and creeping things are awakened into life, demonstrating their living power and effect solely from sheer decay. This may rightly be recognized as a wonder of wonders of nature. Yet nature has permitted this because she has found such living multiplication and awakening of life most often within the earth; through the other elements, life is awakened in that form by <term>spiritual seeds</term> <note>An early concept suggesting that life-giving "seeds" or essences are carried through the air or spirit to spark growth in decaying matter.</note>.

The villager’s wife knows best how to prove this with examples; for she can raise no hens for the household unless it happens through the putrefaction of the egg, from which the chick is born.

If bread is placed in honey, ants will grow within it, which is also a singular **miracle of nature** <note>original: *miraculum naturæ*</note> above all others.

### ...of the Ancient Sages.

**Salt of Glory.** In the <term>distillation</term> <note>A process of heating a substance to separate its components based on their volatility.</note> of water, air and fire also pass over first; water follows afterward, and the <term>body</term> <note>original: *Corpus*; refers to the solid, physical residue left after volatile components have evaporated.</note> of the earth remains at the bottom <note>original: *in fundo*; the base of the glass vessel used in the experiment.</note>.

The element of fire—when it is driven into a visible essence—can, through <term>extraction</term> <note>The alchemical method of drawing out a specific essence or "virtue" from a raw material.</note>, have its fire, water, and earth each captured and driven out separately. In the same manner, air dwells within the other three elements. For none among these can dispense with or exist without air: the earth is nothing and can bring forth nothing without air; fire does not burn and has no life without air; water can never promote nor bring forth any single fruit without air.

Furthermore, air can consume nothing, nor dry out any moisture, unless that occurs through the natural heat that dwells within the air. Since heat and warmth are found in the air, the element of fire must indeed be in the air as well. For everything that is hot and dry is associated with the fiery essence. Therefore, no element can dispense with or exist without the others; rather, there is always a mingling

For you should know this: when something is to be born through putrefaction <note>original: *Fäulung*; in alchemy, this refers to a stage where matter is broken down and blackened, which was believed to be necessary before a new, higher form could "grow" from it.</note>, it must happen in this way. Now, if a birth is to follow afterward from this putrefaction, it must ignite and spread itself through a warm property—as the element of fire—for without natural heat, no birth can be demonstrated. If the birth is then to take on a living breath and movement, this cannot happen without the air. For if the air did not also act upon it and prove its part, the first <term>composition</term> <note>original: *Composition*; the initial mixture or "recipe" of elemental ingredients.</note> and the essence from which the birth should follow would suffocate and perish within itself for lack of air. # Of the Great Stone

Fire / the water was incorporated into the earth, because they must necessarily stand together in a uniformly grasped harmony <note>original: *Concordanz*; meaning a state of agreement or symmetrical balance between the elements.</note> if life is otherwise to follow.

Thus was **Adam** first awakened, built together, and born out of earth, water, air, and fire; out of soul, spirit, and body; and finally out of **Mercury, Sulphur, and Salt.**

So too **Eve**, the first woman and mother of us all, is a partaker of the same, because she was born out of **Adam**, and thus **Eve** originated and was built from **Adam**; note this well. And to return to the subject of **putrefaction** <note>original: *Putrefaction vnnd Fäulung*; the process of decay or breaking down which alchemists believed was the essential first step to any new growth or transformation.</note> and rotting, the seeking master of the art and the wise student of philosophy should know that in the same way, no metallic seed can work, nor multiply in any way, unless such metallic seed is first brought to perfect rot in and through itself alone, without any foreign addition or mixture. For just as all vegetable and animal seeds—as has been explained and indicated—cannot bring forth any <term>augmentation</term> <note>The alchemical process of increasing the power, quantity, or quality of a substance, often compared to the growth of a plant from a seed.</note> without rotting, understand then also

### of the ancient Sages.

of metals / which rot <note>original: *Fäulung*; the process of decomposition that alchemists believed was necessary to release the "seed" of a substance.</note> must now reach its perfect operation through the help of the elements. This is not because the elements themselves are the seed—as has been sufficiently made known before—but rather that the metallic seed (which sprang forth through the heavenly, sidereal <note>Relating to the stars or celestial influences.</note>, and elemental essence and entered into a physical body) must be further brought into such rot and destruction through the elements.

Note also this: that wine possesses a volatile spirit. For in its distillation, the spirit goes first and the <term>phlegma</term> <note>original: *Phlegma*; in early chemistry, this refers to the watery, tasteless, or inactive part of a liquid left over after the "spirit" has been removed.</note> last. However, as soon as it is turned into vinegar through prolonged warmth, its spirit is no longer as volatile as it was before. For in the distillation of vinegar, the water or <term>aquosity</term> <note>original: *aquositet*; the watery, liquid component of a substance.</note> goes first, and the <term>spiritus</term> <note>The active, essential, or "soul-like" essence of a substance.</note> last. 

And although the material before one's eyes is the same as was previously in the vessel, it has nevertheless acquired a very different property, because it is no longer wine. Instead, it has been <term>transmuted</term> <note>original: *transmutiret*; fundamentally changed from one nature into another.</note> and become vinegar through the <term>putrefaction</term> <note>The alchemical stage of "blackening" or breaking down a substance through gentle, steady heat.</note> and rotting caused by constant warmth; and everything that is extracted with the wine or its spirit

### Of the Great Stone

and is circulated <note>A process of continuous distillation where the vapor returns to the liquid, used to refine or "mature" a substance.</note> / has a very different property and effect than that which is extracted with **vinegar** <note>original: *Aceto*</note> / For if the **Glass of Antimony** <note>original: *Vitrum Antimonii*; a translucent substance produced by roasting antimony ore, historically used as a powerful and often dangerous emetic.</note> is extracted with wine or **spirit of wine** <note>original: *Spiritu vini*; high-proof alcohol.</note> / it causes many stools and bowel movements / as well as great vomiting / for it is still a **poison** <note>original: *Venenum*</note> / and its venom is not yet destroyed and broken / but if the glass made from antimony / is extracted with good distilled vinegar / it gives a beautiful **extraction** of a very deep color / then the vinegar is drawn off in a **water bath** <note>original: *Marienbadt* or *Bain-Marie*; a vessel used for gentle, indirect heating.</note> / and the yellow powder / which remains / is well **sweetened** <note>In alchemy, "sweetening" or "dulcifying" refers to washing a substance with water to remove corrosive acids or salts, rather than adding sugar.</note> through frequent distillation of common water / so that all the **acidity** <note>original: *acetositas*</note> is removed from it / then one has a sweet powder / which no longer causes bowel movements / but is a wonderful medicine to use / which proves its worth with wonder and may rightly be considered a **miracle of medicine** <note>original: *miraculum Medicinæ*</note>.

This wonderful powder also dissolves in a **damp place** <note>original: *in loco humido*; alchemists often left salts in damp cellars to allow them to naturally absorb moisture and turn into oil or liquid.</note> into a **liquid** <note>original: *liquorem*</note> / which also performs its work without any pain or ache in **surgical matters** <note>original: *Chyrurgiadas*; referring to the treatment of external wounds or sores.</note> / enough of that for now.

As a final instruction upon this departure, you should by all means understand that you must set up the celestial scales. Upon one side, place **Aries** <note>original: *Wider*</note>, **Taurus** <note>original: *Stier*</note>, **Cancer** <note>original: *Krebs*</note>, **Scorpio** <note>original: *Scorpion*</note>, and **Capricorn** <note>original: *Steinbock*</note>. Of the Great Stone

## The Tenth Key.

<image-desc>
An inverted equilateral triangle containing a central circle. 
Around the outside of the triangle is a German sentence: "From Hermes <note>original: "Hermogene" refers to Hermes Trismegistus, the legendary mythical founder of alchemy.</note> I am born / And from sinfulness sprouted / And have come back to honor."
Inside the triangle:
- Top Left: The Hebrew word "In Three" <note>original: בשלשה (B'shlosha)</note>.
- Top Right: The Hebrew word "One" <note>original: אחד (Echad)</note>.
- Center Circle: The Hebrew name of God, "Yahweh" <note>The Tetragrammaton יהוה</note>.
- Bottom: The alchemical symbol for **Mercury** (☿) above the Hebrew word "In Four" <note>original: בארבע (B'arba). This likely refers to the four elements or the progression from the Trinity to the material world.</note>.
</image-desc>

->From Hermes I am born<-
->And from sinfulness sprouted<-
->And have come back to honor<-

In our Stone—made through me and long before my time—are enclosed all **Elements** <note>The four classical elements: Earth, Air, Fire, and Water.</note>, all mineral and metallic forms, indeed all qualities and properties of the entire world. For within it must be found the greatest and most powerful heat; because through its great internal fire, the cold body of **Saturn** <note>In alchemical symbolism, Saturn represents the metal Lead.</note> is warmed, and through such inflammation, it is changed into the very best **Gold**.

<meta>catchword: It [Es]</meta>

<summary>This page presents the Tenth Key of the alchemical process, using a triangular diagram to illustrate the divine and elemental origins of the Philosopher's Stone. It emphasizes that the Stone contains all worldly properties and uses its internal heat to transmute "cold" lead (Saturn) into gold.</summary>
<keywords>Tenth Key, Hermes Trismegistus, Saturn, Lead, Gold, Elements, Internal Fire, Transmutation, Alchemy</keywords>

--- Page 133 ---
<meta>This page continues the explanation of the Tenth Key of the Great Stone. The previous page established that the Stone contains all elements and properties of the world, specifically noting its internal heat which transforms Saturn (Lead) into Gold. This page shifts to the necessity of internal cold to balance the process.</meta>

->of the ancient Sages.<-

In it must also be found the greatest possible cold; for through its addition, the fiery degree of **Venus** <note>In alchemical symbolism, Venus represents the metal Copper.</note> is tempered, and living **Mercury** <note>Quicksilver; the volatile principle that must be "fixed" or solidified in the work.</note> is coagulated. In the same way, through its solidification, it must become good, enduring **Gold**. The cause of this is that all such properties of our **Matter** <note>original: Materia</note> of the Great Stone are infused by Nature herself. These properties must be boiled out and ripened through the <term>degrees of fire <note>original: gradus ignis; the four traditional stages of increasing heat used in the alchemical furnace.</note></term> until they have attained the highest perfection. This does not happen until Mount **Etna** in **Sicily** <note>A famous active volcano, used here as a metaphor for the intense heat of the alchemical furnace.</note> has burned itself out, and no more cold can be found on the dense, high mountains of the **Hyperboreans** <note>A mythical people of the far north in Greek legend; here representing the extreme cold that must be overcome or integrated.</note>, a place which one may also call **Filictum** <note>A Latin term for a place of ferns; likely an alchemical code for a specific stage of the cooling or "vegetation" of the stone.</note>.

All fruit, if it is plucked before its perfect ripeness, is useless and withers, so that it cannot be used for any profit. Likewise, if the potter does not burn his wares sufficiently with the **Fire** and finish them, the wares will not be fit for use, for they have not been sufficiently ripened by the fire.

<meta>catchword: So [Also]</meta>

<summary>This page concludes the Tenth Key, explaining that the Philosopher's Stone requires a balance of extreme heat and cold to reach perfection. # Of the Great Stone

So also with our **Elixir** <note>The medicinal or transmutative substance sought by alchemists.</note> it must be observed and diligently noted that it be granted its proper time, and that its progress <note>original: *Wolfahrt*; literally "welfare" or "well-being," here referring to the healthy development of the chemical process.</note> not be cut short before its season, lest a false judgment be cast upon it and the blame for its unworthiness be laid upon it. For if the blossom is broken off, one may well consider that no fruit can grow from it thereafter. Therefore, haste is not good for the <term>Mastery <note>original: *Meiſterſchafft*; the completed Great Work or the status of a Master Alchemist.</note></term>, for a hurried man seldom works anything good in the **Art** <note>Alchemy was traditionally called "The Royal Art."</note>, but is rather ruined more than improved by his haste.

Therefore, no seeker should allow himself to be led astray by desire to take out or break off anything before its time, lest the apple fall from him and he be left holding only the empty stem in his hands. For in truth, if our **Stone** is not sufficiently ripened, it will likewise be unable to perform any ripe work.

In the <term>degree of water <note>original: *Waſſergrad*; the first and gentlest stage of heating, often using a water bath (bain-marie).</note></term> the **Matter** <note>original: *Materia*</note> is opened up and unified through <term>putrefaction <note>original: *Fäulung*; the essential stage of decay and blackening (nigredo) that allows for new life to emerge.</note></term>; in the <term>ashes <note>original: *Aſchen*; the second degree of heat, using a bed of ash to provide a steady, moderate temperature.</note></term> it receives the blossom of the fruit; through the <term>sand <note>original: *Sand*; the third degree of heat, using a sand bath for more intense, dry heat.</note></term> all superfluities <note>Excessive or unnecessary moisture and impurities.</note> are dried out; the enduring

A fencer who cannot use his sword well gains no benefit from it, for he has not rightly learned the practice of its usefulness; if another, who can use the sword better than he, comes against him in an encounter, the unskilled man must be defeated. ### Of the Great Stone

When the Medicine and the well-prepared Stone of all Sages—made from the true Virgin's Milk <term>Virgin's Milk: A symbolic alchemical term (Lac Virginis) usually referring to a white, mercurial solvent or a specific stage of the liquid matter in the Great Work</term> and fully completed—then take one part of it. Take three parts of the very best and finest gold, purified and cast through antimony <term>Antimony: A metalloid used by alchemists to refine gold; when melted together, the antimony would consume impurities, leaving "Philosophical Gold" behind</term>. Beat this gold as thin as is humanly possible. 

Place them together into a vessel such as is used for melting metals. Initially, give it a gentle, subtle fire for twelve hours; then let it flow <note>original: "fliessen," meaning to remain in a molten or liquid state</note> continuously for three days and nights. Thus, the purified gold and the Stone become sheer, pure Medicine of a most subtle, spiritual, and penetrating quality. 

For without the ferment of gold <term>Ferment: A substance—in this case, gold—added to the spiritualized Stone to "fix" its power and act as a bridge, allowing it to act upon physical metals</term>, the Stone cannot work effectively or manifest its tincture, for it is otherwise too subtle and piercing. But if it is fermented and combined with its own kind <note>meaning: combined with the physical metal it is meant to represent</note>, then the completed tincture has attained an entry or ingress to work upon other things. 

Then take <note>The following three words are struck through in the original text: "die Medicin von" (the medicine from)</note> one part of the prepared ferment for every thousand parts of molten metal that you wish to tinge <term>Tinge: The alchemical act of "coloring" or transmuting a base metal into a noble one (gold or silver) using a Tincture</term>; know then, in the highest truth of all the world

*The outcome proves the deeds.* <note>original: "Exitus acta probat." A Latin maxim reminding the reader that the truth of the alchemical theory is only found in the successful completion of the physical work.</note>

## Recapitulation of the Great Work

I, Basilius Valentinus, a brother of the Benedictine Order, have previously written a small book and discovered—as the ancients did according to the art of <term>Philosophy</term><note>In this era, "Philosophy" usually refers to "Hermetic Philosophy" or alchemy—the study of the hidden laws of nature and the transformation of matter.</note>—how one should obtain the ancient treasure with which the true sages brought their lives to an advanced old age.

And although—as my conscience shall testify before the Highest in Heaven, to whom all secrets are fundamentally manifest—I have written nothing false, but rather revealed the truth itself so that those who understand would have need of no further light, for the <term>theory</term><note>original: "Theorica"</note> I prescribed to them (which has been confirmed and approved by the <term>practice</term><note>original: "Practica"</note> of my <term>Twelve Keys</term><note>Referring to one of the most famous alchemical texts, "The Twelve Keys of Basil Valentine," which describes the creation of the Philosopher's Stone through twelve allegorical steps.</note>) would have been more than sufficient;

yet the restlessness of my sleep has moved me, through manifold thoughts, to reveal my written book once more by a shorter path, and to refine the light I have kindled through this means, so that every lover of seeking wisdom may have a better vision and clarity toward the desired discovery. Even if

# Stone of the Ancients

...many will think it has been revealed too clearly, and that I have brought many sins upon myself through such an injustice <note>The "injustice" refers to the breaking of alchemical silence—the traditional vow to keep the secrets of the Stone hidden from the unworthy.</note>. Yet, everyone should know that for the stubborn it will still be difficult enough to find what they seek, but for the chosen it will be clear and easy. Therefore, my successor, obey my voice and follow my teaching, and you will find the right path to the sheepfold <note>A biblical metaphor common in alchemical texts representing the entry into the true knowledge of God and Nature.</note>.

I have written nothing more than that to which I myself will be a witness after my death and the resurrection of my flesh.

You shall learn the "Short Path" <note>The "Short Path" or *Via Brevis* was a sought-after alchemical method supposedly faster and more direct than the traditional, lengthy "Long Path" (*Via Longa*).</note> faithfully and simply from the following report; for my words are intended for simplicity and not for great, magnificent adornment.

I have stated and shown that all things are composed and made from three substances: namely, from <term>Mercury</term>, <term>Sulphur</term>, and <term>Salt</term> <note>original: "Mercurio, Sulphure vnd Sale." # On the Salt of the Philosophers
## I will also present a perspective to you.

Salt makes things **fixed** <note>original: "fix." A substance that is stable and resists the action of fire without evaporating.</note> and **volatile** <note>original: "flüchtig." A substance that can be evaporated or transformed into vapor by heat.</note>, according to how it is ordered and prepared in its state. For the spirit from the **Salt of Tartar** <note>original: "Salze des Tartari." A salt derived from the crusty deposits in wine barrels; highly valued by alchemists as a "vegetable" salt.</note>, if it is driven out by itself without any additives, makes all metals **volatile** through **resolution** <note>original: "resolution." The process of dissolving a solid substance into a liquid state.</note> and **putrefaction** <note>original: "putrefaction." A period of breakdown or fermentation seen as essential for spiritualizing matter.</note>, and opens them into true **living** or **running Mercury** <note>original: "Mercurium vivum oder currentem." Quicksilver or liquid mercury, the fundamental metallic principle.</note>, just as my practical instructions report. The **Salt of Tartar** <note>original: "Sal des Weinsteins." Another name for the salt derived from wine deposits.</note> in itself also **fixes** <note>original: "figirt." To make a substance stable and resistant to heat.</note> powerfully, especially when the heat from **quicklime** <note>original: "lebendigen Kalck." Calcium oxide, which generates intense heat when reacting with moisture.</note> is incorporated into it; for they both have a wonderful degree for fixing. Thus, the **Vegetable Salt** from wine can **fix** and make **volatile** both at once, depending on the preparation for which it is to be used; which is indeed a secret of nature and a marvel of the **Philosophical Art** <note>original: "Philosophischen Kunst." A standard term for the practice of alchemy.</note>.

## ADDITION <note>original: "ADDITIO." An appendix or supplementary note providing further clarification.</note>

Now when you have the **material** <note>original: "Materia."</note> in this way, then take heed thereafter only of the **fire** <note>original: "Fewer."</note>, as to how you should govern it; for in the end, everything depends most on that, at the conclusion of the work. For our fire is a common fire, and our **furnace** <note>original: "Ofen."</note> is a common furnace. And although those who preceded me have written that our fire is not a common fire, I tell you in truth that they kept it hidden according to their manner and mystery, because the material is simple and the work modest, which is promoted and revealed through the governance of the fire alone.

Lamp-fire with **spirit of wine** <note>original: "Spiritu vini." Highly distilled alcohol used as a clean-burning fuel.</note> is of no use, for an excessive cost is incurred by it. **Horse manure** <note>original: "Roßmist." Historically used in alchemy to provide a gentle, steady, moist heat for the "digestion" of substances.</note> is a ruin, and the material cannot be completed through any perfect **degrees** <term>degrees: (original: "gradus") refers to the specific stages or intensities of heat required as the alchemical work progresses.</term>.

### [The Stone] of the Ancients

...[spread], which no herb is to be despised; yet this [mineral medicine] is far preferred over them in virtue, power, effect, and capability.

When **mercury** <term>mercury</term> <note>original: "Quecksilber"</note> is also saturated with **living sulfur** <term>living sulfur</term> <note>original: "lebendigem Schwefel"; in alchemy, "living" or "philosophical" sulfur refers to the active, combustible principle of a substance, purified of impurities.</note> along with **antimony** <note>original: "Antimonio"</note>, then given a good fire for several hours in a **wind furnace** <term>wind furnace</term> <note>original: "Windofen"; a furnace constructed to create a natural or forced draft to reach the high temperatures necessary for smelting and chemical transformation.</note>, and what remains has its salt extracted with **distilled vinegar** <note>original: "distillirtem Essig"</note>, then you have the **Salt of the Philosophers** <note>original: "Sal Philosophorum"</note>. This medicine alone is sufficient to cure **quartan fever** <term>quartan fever</term> <note>original: "Quartan"; a malarial fever where the paroxysm occurs every fourth day.</note> and all other types of fevers.

## Concerning Arsenic.

**ARSENIC** is associated with **Mercury** and **Antimony** like a **bastard** <note>original: "Banckhardt"; an illegitimate child, implying arsenic is related to these substances but considered "lesser" or "unpure" in the alchemical hierarchy.</note> in their friendship / it is poisonous and volatile in its entire being / just like the previous two / in its color it appears white, yellow, and red in its **manifestation** <note>original: "Offenbarung"; the outward appearance or physical revelation of the substance.</note> on the outside / but on the inside it is adorned with all colors / just like its metals / which it was forced to abandon through the **violence of the fire** / It is **sublimated** <note>original: "sublimirt"; the process where a solid turns into a gas and then back into a solid, used for purification.</note> by itself without additives / and also with additives in many different ways / However, when it is driven up through salt and **iron** <note>original: "Martem"; Mars is the alchemical symbol and name for iron.</note> / it looks as transparent as a crystal / But its poison still remains within it / and it is not at all fit for the **augmentation** <note>original: "Zusatz"; the addition of an alchemical substance to a base metal to initiate change.</note> of metals / because the smallest quantity cannot be found in it to transform metals.

## Concerning Vinegar.

In Alchemy and Medicine, almost nothing fruitful can be prepared for which one would not require the help of Vinegar. Therefore, I did not wish to deprive it of its due praise, but rather to attach it to this book as an *accidental property* <term>accidens: In historical philosophy, an "accident" is a quality or attribute that does not belong to the essential nature of a thing but is nonetheless attached to it.</term>. In Alchemy, it proves its worth in the decay or *putrefaction* <term>putrefaction: The alchemical stage of decomposition, often represented by the color black, where a substance is broken down to be reborn.</term> of metals and mineral things. It is also used for the extraction of their essence, color, and tincture <note>original: "Tinctur"; a concentrated liquid extract containing the medicinal or alchemical "soul" of a substance.</note>, once they have been previously prepared—just as the Spirit of Wine <note>referring to alcohol.</note> is used to *extract* <term>extrahirn: From the Latin "extrahere," meaning to draw out or pull out the vital components of a plant or mineral.</term> from vegetable things.

If you succeed in this step, neither **Sol** <note>Gold.</note> nor **Luna** <note>Silver.</note> will be able to keep you from gaining great wealth.

The first Salt is hidden in the grapevine wood / which, when burnt to ashes / and a lye <note>A liquid solution made by soaking wood ashes in water, traditionally used for soap-making or extracting salts.</note> is made from it / so that its Salt is drawn out / and coagulated / that is the first Salt.

The third Salt is this / when the wine is distilled / it leaves dregs <note>original: "feces"</note> behind / which are burnt to powder / then one can also draw out its Salt with warm water / and yet each Salt has its own particular property and efficacy <note>original: "efficatio"</note> / but in the Center they stand in a concordance <note>original: "Concordantz"</note> / for they all come from one root. Likewise, there are also three types of Mercury / and three types of Sulfur / The first fatness <note>The "fatness" or "oil" represents the alchemical Principle of Sulfur.</note> is made from the vine-stock / the second oil from the red Tartar / the third is the Oil of Wine <note>original: "Oleum vini"</note>.

# Stone of the Ancients

...true **potable gold** <term>potable gold: Latin: *aurum potabile*. A legendary alchemical medicine where gold is dissolved into a drinkable liquid, believed to be a universal cure and a restorer of youth.</term> can be made. 
But few know how to prepare the spirit of wine <note>Highly concentrated ethanol or brandy.</note> correctly / much less how to fathom its true properties.

Many paths have been tried to obtain the spirit of wine without adulteration / such as through various **instruments** and distilling through metallic serpents <note>Coiled cooling tubes, often made of copper or tin, used in a still to condense vapors.</note> / and many strange inventions / also through sponges, paper, and other means. Some have let rectified brandy freeze in the great cold / believing the **phlegm** <note>The watery, inactive, or "tasteless" part of a distilled liquid.</note> would turn to ice / and the **spirit** would remain liquid and accessible / but there is no truth in any of these methods. 

However, the right way to obtain it / I shall teach you at the end of my practical instructions / for it must be subtle / penetrating without any **phlegm**, but entirely pure / airy and volatile / so that even the air can draw it to itself in a magnetic fashion / therefore one must store it well to keep it. Its effect is penetrating and powerful / and its uses are manifold.

There are three things / which are the noblest creatures in the world / which also love one another wondrously and intensely.

## Repetition of the Great

Among the <term>animals <note>original: *Animalibus*</note></term> it is Man /
for out of his <term>mumia <note>original: *Mumia*. In alchemical and Paracelsian thought, this is the vital life force or "balsam" inherent in the living body, believed to have great healing and transformative power.</note></term>, the **Animal Stone**

# Of the Stone of the Ancients.

For these stones rejuvenate humans and livestock; they rectify <term>leprous metals <note>original: *aussetzigen Metallen*. In alchemy, base metals like lead or tin were considered "sick" or "leprous" versions of gold and required "healing" via the Philosophers' Stone to reach perfection.</note></term> and make all unfruitful trees and growing herbs fertile and of a new birth, such that no human reason can well grasp or comprehend it.

When a rectified <term>water of life <note>original: *Aqua vitæ*. A term for concentrated ethanol or distilled brandy.</note></term> or brandy is ignited with a flame, the **Mercury** <note>original: *Mercurius*. Here representing the volatile, watery principle of the substance.</note> and the **Vegetable Sulfur** <note>original: *Sulphur Vegetabile*. The oily, flammable principle of the plant.</note> separate from one another; the sulfur burns quite hotly, for it is a pure fire, while the delicate Mercury flies away into the air and returns again into its <term>chaos <note>original: *Chaos*. In alchemical theory, this refers to the unformed, primal state of matter or the surrounding atmosphere from which elements are drawn.</note></term>.

But he who can enclose and capture this fiery spirit may boast that he has achieved a <term>knightly feat <note>original: *Ritterstück*. A masterpiece or a deed worthy of a knight.</note></term> in the **chemical field-battle**, and has won a **victory** <note>original: *Victoriam*</note> and **triumph** <note>original: *Triumph*</note> of much knowledge with honor; for this vegetable fiery sulfur is alone the single key to extract the metallic and mineral sulfur from their <term>bodies <note>original: *Corporibus*. The physical, solid form of a mineral before its "soul" or essence is extracted.</note></term>.

With this I conclude my book, and what is written therein is not out of delusion <note>original: *wehnen*. Meaning false opinion or mere imagination; the author asserts his text is based on true experimental knowledge.</note> /

All seekers of the art of desired wisdom should take good note—and from me, a man of the spirit to whom the Most High has granted much of nature to be revealed through persistent prayer, consider this well: that the body of man is governed by and is an imitator of the great world. # Of the Great Mystery <note>original German: "Heimligkeit"</note>
## of the World and its Medicine.

The heavenly revelation of the two kinds of light <note>Likely referring to the "Light of Nature" and the "Light of Grace," a common concept in Paracelsian philosophy.</note> and all the secrets of the entire medical art and pharmacy—which is a wonderful **instrument** <note>original Latin: "Organum"</note> of the **microcosm** <note>original German: "kleine Welt"; the human being viewed as a miniature reflection of the universe.</note>, to be used both inside and outside (that is, within the body and for external common wounds, as well as mostly for those arising from bodily injuries and originating from internal afflictions)—comes forth from a single root. However, they require different preparation and use; for that which is internal cannot be compared to the external, nor the external to the internal, regarding the readiness of their effects. Yet, in their form and matter, they are compared by a single judgment.

And so that I may rightly inform my fellow Christians, I openly confess that there are two medicines which heal everything without distinction, and both are made by One. The first is called **PHALAIA** <note>An alchemical term for a universal internal remedy or panacea.</note>, which serves internally. The other is called by its true name **ASA** <note>An alchemical term for a universal external remedy.</note>, a cure and removal for external ailments, and

# Concerning the Great Secret /

...absurdity, delusion, and emptiness, and can be neither considered nor proven to be a perfect **art** <term>Kunst: In this period, "Art" refers to a practical skill, science, or technical discipline, such as alchemy or medicine.</term>. Just so it is here, regarding the knowledge of other **secrets of Nature** <note>original: "Heimligkeiten der Natur" — these are the hidden properties or "occult" forces that govern how materials and living things behave.</note> which cannot be grasped with the hands—unlike the forming of the aforementioned crafts—but instead require one to learn more through other broader means. These means seem strange to the inexperienced because no **experience** <note>original: "Experienz"</note> is demanded of them.

Yes, I say, it requires much excellent diligence to bring forth, research, and proclaim that which lies hidden within them and what can be made from them. This must then occur through the dividing and **separation** <term>Separation: In alchemy, this is the fundamental process of chemically or spiritually isolating the "pure" essence of a substance from its "impure" dross.</term>, so that Nature is disassembled and its types of good and evil are learned—what every nature is capable of in its **general center** <note>original: "Centro generaliter" — the essential core common to all things in a certain category.</note> and what proceeds from it in its **specific form** <note>original: "specie" — the individual or particular manifestation of a thing.</note>.

Therefore, the great and small world—indeed, all things that grow from them and are found alongside them—are to be compared to a round circle, in which the **center** <note>original: "Centrum"</note> is **in the middle** <note>original: "in medio"</note>. No matter which way one turns the circle, it remains round at all ends, and the center remains unmoved.

<summary>The author explains that understanding Nature requires more than theory; it requires the "separation" of substances to find their hidden essence. He introduces the metaphor of the circle and its center to describe the unchanging core of the Macrocosm and Microcosm.</summary>

->of the World / and its Medicine.<-

Thus, the true **philosopher** <note>original: "Philosophus" — in this context, someone who studies nature through alchemy and early chemistry.</note> must also rightly know the **center** <note>original: "Centrum"</note> of every **matter** <note>original: "Materia"</note>, which must remain unmoved within every being. For now, I say this for your observation: I take a natural thing before me and unlock it through a key which is the means of decomposition, and I search within it through the **trial** <note>original: "Proba"</note> of fire—which is a master of all **examinations** <note>original: "Examina"</note>—to see what can be made of it.

There I often find so many wonders and properties that I had not previously touched upon in thought, much less experienced. <meta>This page continues the author's argument that alchemical truths must be proven through physical results rather than just theory. ### Of the Great Mystery

Unbelief, through a dull understanding, has not been able to grasp [this]; but whoever wishes to teach me better through writing should not satisfy me with words alone, but the proof should be made demonstrable alongside them. # The Philosopher speaks
->thus:<-

O Sun / a king of this world /
The Moon <note>original: "Luna"</note> sustains your lineage /
Mercury <note>original: "Mercurius"</note> unites you firmly /
<margin>Spirit</margin> Without Venus’s favor you achieve nothing /
Who has chosen Mars <note>original: "Martem"</note> as her husband /
Let Jupiter’s <note>original: "Jovis"</note> grace not be lost /
So that Saturn <note>original: "Saturnus"</note>, old and gray /
Manifests himself in many colors /
From black to white / from white to red /
<note>These colors refer to the "Great Work" of alchemy: *nigredo* (blackening/death), *albedo* (whitening/purification), and *rubedo* (reddening/perfection).</note>
On stilts he climbs into death /
And runs straight toward life /
Then he has peace and good rest.
Arise, green woman / oh dear son
Antimony <note>original: "Antimonium," a metalloid used in purifying gold.</note> help to support me /
And noble salt / help me to resist /
So that the worms do not consume me.

---

## RIDDLE <note>original: "ÆNIGMA"</note>

Five books were prepared for us /
By Moses alone / through God’s composition /
A lesser number follows his books /
What was commanded there to his people /

<meta>catchword: Three</meta>

<summary>A poem voiced by "The Philosopher" personifies the planets as alchemical agents performing a transformation from death (black) to life (red), followed by a riddle regarding the books of Moses.</summary>

## Of the Great Mystery
<note>original: "Heimligkeit." This refers to a hidden secret or an esoteric truth known only to initiates.</note>

Three Patriarchs, mute /
Explain it together in a single sum /
One witness speaks with the loudest voice /
He who is worth nothing is empty in mind /
Fifty is a greater number than five /
And yet they are only two everywhere /
A thousand conclude it all at once /
Whoever understands it rightly is truly rich /
Five things in life reveal this /
And five in death were also there /
Four pronounce the judgment /
The one alone settles the struggle <note>original: "Straus," meaning a dispute, fight, or a difficult task.</note>.

---

### VITRIOL <note>original: "VICTRIOLUM." Vitriol (metal sulfates) was a fundamental substance in alchemy. Its name was often treated as an acronym for "Visita Interiora Terrae Rectificando Invenies Occultum Lapidem" (Visit the interior of the earth; by rectification you will find the hidden stone).</note>

From Venus’s <note>Venus represents copper in the alchemical tradition.</note> love make yourself a stone /
And drive out from it the spirit alone /
Red, thick and murky / just like blood /
Which completely breaks Mars <note>original: "Martem." Mars represents iron.</note> apart /
From this make a stone again /
Exactly matching the one before /
Within it lies great art and wonder /
To clothe the white Moon <note>original: "Luna." Moon represents silver.</note> who is naked /
The Sun <note>original: "Sol." Sun represents gold.</note> can also achieve nothing more without it /
That makes Mercury <note>original: "Mercurium." Mercury represents quicksilver.</note> a total fool <note>original: "Affen," literally "ape." To "make an ape" of someone meant to mock or surpass them.</note> /
If you can arrange the matter rightly /
Then they will pass the judgment.

<meta>catchword: ANTI-</meta>

<summary>This page presents two alchemical poems: a numerical riddle concerning "The Great Mystery" and a symbolic instruction for processing Vitriol to transform base metals like iron and copper into a "stone" capable of affecting silver and gold.</summary>
<keywords>Great Mystery, Patriarchs, Vitriol, Venus, copper, Mars, iron, Luna, silver, Sol, gold, Mercury, quicksilver, alchemical transformation</keywords>

->of the World/ and its Medicine.<-

### ANTIMONY <note>original: "ANTIMONIUM." A metallic element that was highly significant in alchemy for its ability to purify gold and its perceived medicinal properties.</note>

On my own you can make nothing,
But I serve for metallic matters.
Only add that which I purge,
And turn Mercury <note>original: "Mercurium." The alchemical principle of fluidity and transformation.</note> into an oil.
Let Mars <note>original: "Martem." The alchemical name for Iron.</note> struggle with the Eagle <note>In alchemical texts, the "Eagle" usually represents a volatile substance, often sal ammoniac, or the process of sublimation where solids are turned into vapor.</note>;
Then unite us both together,
So that a treaty is established.
Then I shall prove, for all to see,
How I can conquer the Moon <note>original: "Luna." The alchemical name for Silver.</note>,
So that from silver, gold can be produced.

### COMMON SULFUR <note>original: "SULPHUR Vulgare." Sulfur was one of the three "primordial principles" (alongside Mercury and Salt) and was associated with heat, soul, and combustibility.</note>

I am a pure and burning fire,
Come from monstrous mountains.
I destroy metals completely,
Yet I can nourish their kind with my essence.
If I am prepared with care,
I have a use in medicine.
But whoever has set his mind
To hope for profit from metals
May look for it elsewhere;
From me, he will find nothing for that.
Only he who wishes to refine <note>original: "gradirn." From "graduating," a technical term for a process intended to improve the quality, color, or purity of a metal.</note>
Can draw something of value from me.

->Of the Great Secret<-

## ARSENIC <note>original: "ARSENICUM."</note>

My name is Smelter-fume <note>original: "Hüttenrauch." A historical term for arsenic trioxide, which was collected as a white soot or "smoke" from the flues of smelting furnaces.</note>,
And I am a poisonous, evil smoke.
I have nothing more to claim for myself,
Because I have left my body <note>original: "Corpus." In alchemy, this refers to the solid, physical state of a substance.</note>;
Therefore no one can force me again
To be brought back into a physical form.
But when I lose my poison
Through Art <note>The "Art" refers to the practice of alchemy/chemistry.</note> and the correct manipulations,
Then I can cure people and cattle,
Oftentimes leading them out of evil sickness.
Yet prepare me rightly and take good care
That you keep good watch over me,
Otherwise I am poison and remain poison,
Which stabs many a heart in the body.

## SAL AMMONIAC <note>original: "SAL ARMONIAC." Ammonium chloride, a volatile salt used in alchemy to dissolve metals and aid in sublimation.</note>

When my wings are broken off <note>A metaphor for removing the substance's volatility so it can no longer "fly" (vaporize) away.</note>,
And I am prepared for the water-bath
With my enemy, the Earth,
Then such a thing can become of me
That I break the form of metals
And drive them over <note>Refers to the process of sublimation or distillation, where the metal is carried over in a vaporous state.</note> with force.
Tartar <note>original: "Tartarus." Potassium bitartrate, a byproduct of winemaking used as a flux or reagent.</note> must be present there,
Then a fine Mercury <note>original: "Mercurius." Here likely referring to a "philosophical mercury" or a purified metallic essence rather than common quicksilver.</note> will come of it.

<meta>catchword: More</meta>

->of the World and its Medicine.<-

More I cannot bring to you,
For Sun and Moon <note>original: "Sol vnd Luna." In alchemical symbolism, the Sun represents Gold and the Moon represents Silver.</note> are not within me.

### COMMON SALT <note>original: "SAL COMMUNE." Sodium chloride, or table salt, viewed by alchemists as a fundamental preservative and purifying agent.</note>

I am called a wonderful balsam,
What is clearly found within the Eagle <note>The "Eagle" is an alchemical symbol often representing Sal Ammoniac or the process of sublimation (the rising of vapors).</note>,
Resides in me also, quite the same.
Otherwise, I make no metals rich,
Except that I alone break them down,
Purge and cleanse their lineage <note>Refers to the purification of metallic substances to reach their "essential" state.</note>,
And also extract from them their color and Tincture <note>The "Tincture" is the concentrated essence or soul of a substance, capable of transmuting or healing.</note>,
Provided I am sweet and not at all sour.
Spirit of wine <note>original: "Weingeist." High-proof alcohol or ethanol.</note> causes me fear and woe;
This produces Drinkable Gold <note>original: "Aurum potabile." A legendary medicine made from gold dissolved in a liquid, believed to grant longevity.</note>.

### SALTPETER <note>Potassium nitrate, a key ingredient in gunpowder and strong acids (like nitric acid) used to dissolve metals.</note>

I am a wondrous salt on Earth,
The likes of me will scarcely be seen.
Without me, one can achieve almost nothing;
I must help to settle all matters.
My Eagle cannot do without me
When he intends to "roast" metals <note>Refers to the chemical process of calcination or oxidation using heat.</note>.
Common salt can complete nothing without me,
Even if it wished to turn away from me entirely.
My form is simply like pure ice,
Wherein you find a potent spirit.

# Of the Great Secret, etc.

->In both of these, Nature<-
->Reveals herself in many a form.<-

### TARTARUS <note>original: "Tartarus." Also known as "Cream of Tartar" (potassium bitartrate), this substance is a naturally occurring sediment found in wine barrels. Alchemists valued it as a "vegetable salt" used to create powerful solvents.</note>

I call myself vegetable <note>original: "Vegetabilisch." In alchemy, substances were categorized into the Mineral, Vegetable, or Animal kingdoms. Tartar was considered the "salt" of the vegetable kingdom because it comes from grapes.</note>,
The **strong wines** know me well;
When the other salts add me to their company,
I lead them to the key <note>The "key" refers to the ability to open or dissolve solid matter, a fundamental step in alchemical transformation.</note>;
Through my spirit, they exert such power
That they break down all metals completely.
I bring this with me out of the earth,
So that they all become mercury <note>original: "Quecksilber." Here, it refers to the "mercury of the philosophers"—reducing a solid metal back into its fluid, "prime" state so it can be purified.</note>;
No other plant in the world can do this.
Nature has indeed granted this to me—
Such friendship and such great deeds
That not one man in a thousand can fathom them.

# Of the Mastery of the Seven Planets / their Essence / Properties / Power and Course / also their hidden Secrets and Wonders / etc.

---

## Of the First and highest Planet, Saturn. <note>original: "Saturno." In the traditional Ptolemaic system, Saturn was the "highest" planet because it was the furthest from Earth and the slowest moving of the seven known celestial bodies.</note>

I come from the highest heaven /
And shine above, wide and far /
So that I can barely be discerned /
In my mind I am a man of wonders / <note>original: "Wunderman." This suggests Saturn is an enigmatic, strange, or miraculous figure.</note>
I have a most melancholy nature / <note>original: "Melancholisch Art." According to the "Four Humors" theory of the time, Saturn was the source of "black bile," causing a temperament that was cold, dry, and prone to deep thought or gloom.</note>
From which I have my grey beard /
Saturday was assigned to me / <note>original: "Sambstag." The English "Saturday" and the German "Samstag" both derive from the planet's association with the Sabbath and Saturn himself.</note>
Just as it took its name from me /
My course is a complete circular ring /
Which I complete in thirty years / <note>Saturn takes approximately 29.5 Earth years to orbit the sun; this was remarkably accurate for the period.</note>
Five days and six hours belong to it as well /
Yet I can have no rest /
Capricorn and Aquarius /
These two are my subjects / <note>In traditional astrology, every zodiac sign is "ruled" by a planet; Saturn rules both the Goat (Capricorn) and the Water-bearer (Aquarius).</note>
A beautiful **GARNET** stone, clear and bright / <note>original: "GRANATH-stein." Precious gems were often assigned to planets to be used as talismans.</note>
My angel ORIPHIEL gave it to me. <note>In Western esotericism and Agrippa’s occult philosophy, Oriphiel is the Archangel associated with the planet Saturn.</note>

<summary>This page introduces the astrological section of the text, featuring a personified poem about Saturn. It describes the planet's physical orbit (30 years), its association with the melancholy temperament, the zodiac signs of Capricorn and Aquarius, and its presiding archangel, Oriphiel.</summary>
<keywords>Saturn, Astrology, Melancholy, Saturday, Capricorn, Aquarius, Garnet, Oriphiel, Seven Planets</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the personified monologue of Saturn. On the previous page, Saturn introduced himself as the highest planet, ruler of time, and the signs of Capricorn and Aquarius. # Of Properties

<meta>catchword: But</meta>

I sit and calculate day and night,
How I may obtain much wealth,
Acting just like the old misers do,
Who can never become [truly] rich;
Therefore my art is called Astronomy, <note>In this context, "Astronomy" refers to the "Astronomy of the Earth"—an old term for Alchemy, which studied the "stars" or virtues hidden within metals.</note>
To calculate what color I may be;
There you find black, white, yellow, and red, <note>original: "schwartz/ weiß/ gelb vnd roht." These are the four primary stages of the Alchemical Great Work: Nigredo (blackness), Albedo (whiteness), Citrinitas (yellowness), and Rubedo (redness).</note>
But they do not endure in times of need.
My spirit is sweet, cold as ice,
In robbing and stealing I have praise. <note>In traditional astrology, Saturn was considered a "malefic" planet, influencing people toward melancholy, greed, and theft.</note>
But when the spirit is bound,
Then I also do what I promise,
I can tame, change, and also bind,
That which otherwise leaves everything behind.
**FAITH** <note>original: "*FIDES*."</note>, the virtuous one, teaches this,
How I was converted in my old age;
I threw my stilts away from me, <note>Saturn/Cronus was frequently depicted as an old man on stilts or crutches, symbolizing the slow, hobbling movement of the furthest planet and the infirmity of old age.</note>
So that I went my way without them.
A red oil then came from it,
Manifestly **through extraction** <note>original: "*Per extractionem*."</note>,
Distilled thereafter quite pure and bright,
That bound **Mercury** <note>original: "*Mercurium*."</note> soon and quickly,
Making its spirit so fixed and steadfast,
Just like the fine white silver;
When such was turned into a powder,
Escaped from all worry and danger.

<summary>Saturn describes his alchemical transformation from a miserly, cold metal (lead) into a "fixed" substance capable of binding mercury. He uses the metaphor of an old man throwing away his stilts to represent the transition from a base state to a perfected, medicinal oil and powder.</summary>
<keywords>Saturn, Alchemy, Four Stages, Lead, Mercury, Extraction, Distillation, Melancholy, Astronomy</keywords>

<meta>Catchword: The</meta>

But take it entirely to heart,
That I am no common Lead,
But rather of a much different kind,
As my little book reveals to you,
Prepared from a mineral;
If you calculate correctly, you hit the number, <note>Likely referring to the specific numerical proportions or "weights" of the elements required in an alchemical recipe.</note>
I am chosen to put things to the test;
Whoever withstands me wins the day, <note>original: "behelt den Stich." A metaphor from card games meaning to win the trick or hold the advantage; here it refers to the metal's ability to survive the fires of the testing furnace.</note>
And cannot attain the heavens,
From which I have indeed departed,
I summon all that is volatile to justice; <note>In alchemy, Saturn (Lead) is used to "fix" or stabilize "volatile" substances (like Mercury) so they do not evaporate when heated.</note>
Give thanks for that to me, the old, hoary servant.
To our metals I am a death,
And lead them into anguish and need, <note>This refers to "cupellation," a process where lead is melted with other metals. The lead oxidizes and carries away impurities, "killing" the base metals to leave behind pure gold or silver.</note>
But whoever seeks and knows me rightly,
I give enough until his end.
My sharp scythe mows everything down; <note>The scythe is the traditional attribute of Saturn/Cronus as the god of Time (Father Time), who eventually harvests all living things.</note>
With this, I bid the world farewell.

### Of the second Planet
-> J O V E. <- <note>Jove is another name for Jupiter, the king of the gods in Roman mythology and the second-highest planet in the traditional geocentric model.</note>

In ancient times, I made many people learned,
Therefore I am honored as a god,
And especially on Thursday
I was honored above others, <note>The English word "Thursday" (Thor's Day) corresponds to the Latin *Dies Iovis* (Jove's Day) and the German *Donnerstag* (Thunder's Day).</note>

<header>Of the Properties</header>

I always choose the day,
My angel is named **ZACHARIEL**, <note>In Renaissance angelology, Zachariel is the presiding archangel of the planet Jupiter.</note>
At my court I have many people,
Who all desire sustenance from me,
To maintain their house, farm, and table;
My subjects are the Archer and the Fish. <note>These are the zodiac signs Sagittarius and Pisces, which were traditionally ruled by Jupiter in classical astrology.</note>
I pass judgment on both rich and poor,
Whenever I sit in audience;
Therefore the scepter is truly mine to bear.
**RHETORIC** <note>original: **RHETHORICA**; the art of persuasive speech, one of the seven liberal arts associated with Jupiter.</note> guides my tongue,
And places upon me a laurel wreath,
Adorned entirely with roses,
Along with a beautiful golden crown,
Set with the noble **TOPAZ**, <note>original: **TOPASION**; the gemstone traditionally associated with the influence of Jupiter.</note>
So that all may see I am a lord.
**HOPE** <note>original: **SPES**</note> never leaves me;
I clothe in white, yellow, and blue,
And in the wintertime also in gray.
I perform my journey so swiftly,
Completing my circuit in twelve years. <note>This refers to the astronomical orbital period of the planet Jupiter, which takes approximately 11.86 years to circle the sun.</note>
Whoever is known far and wide
Finds my treasure in England; <note>In alchemy, Jupiter represents the metal Tin. In the early modern period, Cornwall, England was the primary source of tin for Europe.</note>
There I must travel across the sea,
Where the water is very salty.
My spirit, my soul, and also my body,
Are separated from each other with subtle art; <note>This refers to the alchemical process of "separation," where a substance is broken down into its three primary components (Sulfur, Mercury, and Salt) to be purified.</note>

And join them back together again,

### Of the third Planet
## MARS. <note>original: *MARTE*; the Latin ablative/dative form of the name. In alchemy, Mars represents the metal Iron.</note>

When I traversed the whole world,
With my long spear held high,
And waged many a war far and wide,
And thereafter returned home again,
I brought with me a fine bounty;
Then all the soldiers chose me
To be a captain in the field.
Everyone wanted to have money from me;
That brought me many a conflict, <note>original: *Straus*; here meaning a skirmish or struggle.</note>
I endured both great fortune and misfortune,
When **SAMAEL**, <note>In traditional Western occultism and Renaissance angelology, Samael is the archangel presiding over the planet Mars.</note> the good angel,
Did not have me in his keeping then.

<meta>This page continues the personification of Mars, who began speaking on page 255. Having introduced his role as a warrior and captain guided by the angel Samael, he now describes his temperament, his astrological associations, and his relationship with Venus.</meta>

# Of Properties

I would have long ago met my end,
And become a roast for hell, <note>original: *Hellebraten*; a colorful early modern German term for someone destined for damnation or the gallows.</note>
But I still offer praise and thanks
To my God, who sent me the angel; <note>A reference to Samael, mentioned on the previous page as the planetary governor of Mars.</note>
And although the **Ram** <note>original: *Widder*; the zodiac sign Aries.</note> and the **Scorpio**
Are my subjects in war, <note>In traditional astrology, Mars is the "ruler" of the signs Aries and Scorpio.</note>
They have nevertheless made many a move
Directed against me during the campaign;
And had they not escaped me,
I would have had them hung from the trees.
My sword is nothing but pure steel, <note>Mars represents the metal Iron, from which steel is made.</note>
And it glitters as clear as a **ruby**,
Which is also set within it;
That same sword devours many a man,
It pierces through manfully and undaunted,
And cares nothing for any person.
In times of peace I am not happy;
My virtue is called **FORTITUDE**, <note>original: *FORTITUDO*; Latin for strength or courage, one of the four cardinal virtues associated here with the planet Mars.</note>
When I am eventually robbed of body and life,
Then I wish to give all my goods
To my lady Queen, high-born:
**Venus**, the most gentle chosen one;
She may then further bestow these gifts
Upon whomever she most wishes to favor.
My noblest garment is entirely red,
Clothed grandly in a purple hue.

<meta>catchword: From which</meta>

<summary>Mars describes his warlike nature, his command over the zodiac signs Aries and Scorpio, and his symbolic sword of steel and ruby. He identifies his primary virtue as Fortitude and declares that upon his death, his "goods" (representing alchemical properties) should pass to Venus.</summary>
<keywords>Mars, Iron, Fortitude, Venus, Aries, Scorpio, Ruby, Alchemy, Planetary Virtues, Red</keywords>

### The 7th Planet.

From which a medicine <note>original: *Artzney*; in this context, it refers to an alchemical tincture or "tincture of Mars" (iron-based medicine).</note> is made,
Prepared freely for many things;
Yet I alone can accomplish nothing,
Unless my companions join themselves to me;
For more of us must be gathered together.
In two years I completely fulfill <note>A reference to the orbital period of Mars, which takes approximately 687 Earth days—nearly two years—to circle the Sun.</note>
And finish my course,
Before I can complete the end.
Upon a **Tuesday** <note>Tuesday is traditionally the day of Mars (derived from the Old English *Tīwesdæg*, "Tiw's Day," associated with the Roman *Dies Martis*).</note> I hold power,
Though many a person pays no heed.
No wings were born to me,
Unless they are specially chosen for me,
So that I travel with peril in air and wind,
Where the mountains are at their highest,
And even where the heavens reach an end,
Which then sends me down again. <note>This likely describes the alchemical process of sublimation and distillation, where a solid metal is "volatized" into vapor (wind/spirit) and then condensed back into a liquid or solid state.</note>
Make of me just such a wind and spirit;
Then promise me entirely to **Venus**; <note>In mythology, Mars and Venus are lovers. Alchemically, this refers to the "marriage" or chemical combination of Iron (Mars) and Copper (Venus).</note>
Then you shall see if it be a lie,
That **Sol** and **Luna** <note>The Sun (representing Gold) and the Moon (representing Silver).</note> are deceived;
Yet I must be made steadfast <note>original: *standhafft*; in alchemical terms, to "fix" a substance so it is no longer volatile and can withstand the heat of the fire.</note> and firm,
If I am to carry out my splendor.

<summary>Mars explains his alchemical role as a "medicine" that requires the aid of other elements to function. He describes his two-year astronomical cycle and the process of alchemical sublimation, ending with his symbolic union with Venus.</summary>
