<meta>This page continues the series on the seven planets and their alchemical/astrological properties. While the previous page focused on Mars (Iron), this page introduces the fourth planet, Sol (the Sun), which represents Gold in the alchemical tradition.</meta>

### On the Properties of the Fourth Planet, SOL. <note>The Sun, representing the metal Gold in alchemy.</note>

<meta>catchword: My</meta>

The whole world with my radiance,
I alone illuminate for everyone;
Both the wicked and the pious I look upon,
And I show no favoritism to any person, <note>A reference to the Sun’s impartiality, similar to the biblical sentiment in Matthew 5:45, where the sun rises on both the evil and the good.</note>
My course I perform so swiftly,
That one cannot see it happening at all;
In three hundred sixty and five days, <note>The length of a solar year.</note>
I am able to complete it entirely.
Also, from my "garment" <note>In alchemical allegory, a planet's "garment" or "clothing" often refers to its outward physical form or its metallic body (Gold).</note> can be made,
A water magnificently prepared, <note>likely a reference to *Aurum Potabile* (potable gold), a legendary alchemical medicine believed to have universal healing powers.</note>
Which in a very short, brief hour,
Makes many a person fresh and 
<margin>healthy,</margin>The key to all secrets,
Depends entirely on me alone,
Which, by the power of God’s grace,
Was assigned to me by the Angel **MICHAEL**, <note>In traditional Christian angelology and astrology, the Archangel Michael is the ruler of the Sun.</note>
My art belongs to **GRAMMAR**, <note>original: *Grammatica*. In the medieval system of the Seven Liberal Arts, each art was often paired with a planet. While Grammar was frequently linked to the Moon, this text explicitly assigns it to the Sun.</note>
Read correctly in the book, and you will find it there;
Then you must admit I am right,
That **JUSTICE** <note>original: *Justicia*.</note> is still alive,
And always still hovers above,
Although she is often deceived.

<summary>The Sun (Sol) describes its role as a universal provider of light and its alchemical potential to create a healing "gold water." It identifies its angelic ruler as Michael and its corresponding liberal art as Grammar, while emphasizing its association with impartial Justice.</summary>
<keywords>Sol, Sun, Gold, Archangel Michael, Grammatica, Justice, Aurum Potabile, Alchemical Medicine, Astrology</keywords>

--- Page 273 ---
<meta>This page continues the poetic descriptions of the seven planets and their alchemical associations. The text concludes the speech of Sol (the Sun/Gold) and introduces Venus (Copper).</meta>

<header>of the 7 Planets.</header>

My Sun carries a carbuncle stone, <note>The carbuncle, a glowing red gemstone, represents the "Red Lion" or the final stage of the alchemical Great Work (Rubedo), associated with the Sun and Gold.</note>
Which is not common to everyone;
When I hold council with potentates,
Sunday is always favorable to me. <note>Sunday is the day traditionally ruled by the Sun in astrology.</note>
Dissolve my fixed body, <note>In alchemy, "fixed" refers to substances that resist the fire; Gold is the most "fixed" of all metals.</note>
And drive a Mercury out from it,
And give to it the woman's blood, <note>original: *Weibes Blut*. In alchemical allegory, this often refers to the "mercurial" or "feminine" solvent required to dissolve the "masculine" gold for the "Chemical Wedding."</note>
From which I myself have my substance;
Feed us both with salt quite rightly, <note>Salt is one of the three "Primes" (Tria Prima) along with Sulfur and Mercury.</note>
Which belongs to my lineage;
Thus you will find in your mind,
Whether I am not a true King. <note>Gold is the "King" of metals.</note>
O Moon, it is my highest plea,
That you would not wish to leave me,
Because Venus is already on the scene,
To put on your finery and adornment;
Although we both are altogether
Adorned by her and become wealthy;
Reflect upon this diligently and constantly,
This I leave to you here as a farewell. <note>original: *Valet*. From the Latin for "farewell."</note>

## On the Fifth Planet
### VENUS.

The Venus-star is my name,
My effect I constantly prove

<meta>This page continues the speech of the fifth planet, Venus, which began at the bottom of the previous page. Having just introduced herself, the personified planet now describes her "children" (those born under her astrological influence) and her various symbolic associations.</meta>

<header>On Properties</header>

With those who are my children,
&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;And created by my blind son,
Who was called Cupid <note>The Roman god of desire, often depicted as blind or blindfolded to represent the randomness of love.</note> from the beginning;
&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;His arrows fly into every land.
Both men and women show this indeed,
&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;Who have surrendered themselves entirely to love.
Music is my free art,
&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;Which I rule through the fire of love;
I prove this with stringed instruments,
&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;Therefore my virtue is called Charity <note>original: *Charitas*, Latin for divine or selfless love.</note>.
I like to nourish myself with good wine,
&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;And must constantly be in joy.
Every pastime that one can imagine,
&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;All flourish at my court.
My heavenly houses are Libra and Taurus <note>In traditional astrology, Venus is the "ruler" of the zodiac signs Libra (the Scales) and Taurus (the Bull).</note>,
&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;Which I carry with me in my coat of arms.
My course in the high firmament
&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;Is completed in three hundred and fifty days <note>While Venus's actual orbital period is about 225 days, early modern texts often used varied figures for the synodic period or symbolic cycles.</note>.
ANAEL <note>In Western occultism, Anael is the archangel typically associated with the planet Venus and the day of Friday.</note> is my angel,
&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;Who protects me from all cunning;
He has given me a noble stone,
&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;Which is commonly called Emerald.
Transparent, green, lovely in its luster,
&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;Such is my color through and through;
Yet within me dwells a red spirit,
&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;I know no name for what it is called <note>This likely refers to an alchemical concept: though the "metal" of Venus (copper) creates green salts (verdigris), it is "red" in its metallic state, suggesting a hidden fiery nature within the cool green exterior.</note>.

<summary>The planet Venus describes her domain over love, music, and joy, identifying her astrological signs (Libra and Taurus) and her archangel, Anael. She concludes with a cryptic alchemical reference to a red spirit hidden within her green gemstone, the emerald.</summary>
<keywords>Venus, Cupid, Music, Charity, Libra, Taurus, Anael, Emerald, Copper, Alchemy</keywords>

--- Page 275 ---
<meta>This page continues the speech of Venus, the personified planet. In the previous section, she discussed her astrological properties and her association with the emerald. Here, she transitions into alchemical allegory, describing her transformation from a base mineral into a powerful medicine through her union with other celestial/metallic principles.</meta>

### of the 7 Planets.

Which I received from my husband/
The praiseworthy, warlike Mars <note>original: *Marti*, the Latin dative for Mars. In alchemy, Venus (copper) and Mars (iron) are often paired.</note>/
Though I was still a mineral/
To be compared entirely to the earth/
But now I am a hot fire/
A medicine powerful and precious/
To which great force has brought me/
That I have attained a new power/
The breath that I blow with might/
When the old dragon <note>In alchemical texts, the "dragon" often represents raw, volatile matter or a powerful acidic solvent.</note> drinks from it/
Then I give him a new soul/
And become quite a magnificent oil/
From which a tincture <note>A "tincture" in alchemy is a potent liquid substance capable of transmuting base metals or healing the body.</note> is prepared/
Highly adorned with its own kind/
Which can dye the white into red <note>This refers to the *rubedo*, the final "reddening" stage of the alchemical Great Work, where the substance reaches its highest perfection.</note>/
Such a fixed powder <term>fixed powder: A substance that has been "fixed" is no longer volatile and can withstand the heat of fire without evaporating.</term> helps in times of need/
My bridegroom is the clear Sun/
From whom I receive joy and delight/
And he from me in return/ <margin>wealth/</margin>
Strength, might, and power and also wealth <note>The German word *Reichtumb* (wealth) is split here, with the suffix in the margin.</note>/
Such inheritance and goods my son receives/
And woos the cold Moon/ <note>original: *Mohn*, likely a pun or archaic variant for *Mond* (Moon) to rhyme with *Sohn* (Son).</note>
Warming the body of Luna/
So that the woman becomes pregnant/
And can leave behind an offspring/
With many, countless good fruits/

<summary>Venus describes her alchemical transformation from a base "mineral" into a "tincture" or "fixed powder" through symbolic unions with Mars and the Sun. This process results in a "son" who then interacts with the Moon to produce further "fruit" or alchemical perfection.</summary>
<keywords>Venus, Mars, alchemy, Tincture, Red Stage, Sun, Luna, Dragon, Medicine</keywords>

<meta>This page concludes the speech of Lady Venus and introduces Mercury, the sixth planet. Following the alchemical journey of the previous page, where Venus (copper) was transformed into a powerful "oil" or "tincture," she now yields the stage to the messenger of the gods, who describes his own volatile and transformative nature.</meta>

<page-num>252</page-num>
# Of Properties

Not one in a thousand will be worthy
Of the great secret that dwells within me,
To which I now say good night;
Friday has its own day, <note>Friday (*Freitag*) is the day of Venus, named after the Germanic goddess Freya, who corresponds to the Roman Venus (*dies Veneris*).</note>
Therefore, just let Lady Venus pass,
She can yet lead you into great wealth.

### Of the sixth Planet
## MERCURY.

Wednesday indeed I have chosen, <note>In the Western tradition, Wednesday is the day of Mercury (French: *Mercredi*; Latin: *dies Mercurii*).</note>
Just as I was originally born;
I soon said good night to the earth
And strived for a higher standing,
I left her then entirely,
Even though she was my mother, <note>In alchemical allegory, the "mother" often represents the Earth or the base mineral ore from which the volatile spirit of mercury is extracted.</note>
And sought friendship in the air;
I soon flew away through wind and vapor.
All the colors that exist in the world
My mother bestowed upon me;
Therefore Raphael <note>The Archangel Raphael is traditionally associated with the planet Mercury, medicine, and the element of Air.</note> gave me a crystal,
To make from it whatever I wish,
Because it takes on all colors
So many that they cannot all be told.
The Maiden and the Twins <note>Referring to the zodiac signs Virgo (the Maiden) and Gemini (the Twins), both of which are ruled by the planet Mercury.</note> have carried it out,
So that the crystal coagulated; <term>coagulated: In alchemy, *coagulatio* is the process of solidifying a liquid or volatile substance, often the counterpart to *dissolutio* (dissolving).</term>
But Raphael gave me the power
And brought me back into Mercury again.

<meta>catchword: Kale (likely "Kalte" or "Cold")</meta>

<summary>This page concludes the personification of Venus, who hints at the hidden wealth of alchemical secrets, and begins the speech of Mercury. Mercury describes his volatile nature, his flight from the "mother" earth into the air, and his ability to manifest all colors through his association with the signs of Virgo and Gemini.</summary>

<keywords>Mercury, Venus, Raphael, alchemical coagulation, Virgo, Gemini, planetary days, color symbolism</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the speech of Mercury, who was introduced on page 252. Having established his celestial origin, Mercury now describes his volatile nature and his essential role in the "Great Work" of alchemy. The header's reference to the "7th planet" likely includes the Sun or Moon in the total count, though Mercury was previously called the sixth.</meta>

<page-num>253</page-num>
<header>of the 7. Planet.</header>

Cold and warm is everything in me/
I stand, then soon run back and forth/
I am quite an adventurous man/
Whom one cannot calculate/
For when I kept a school,
Arithmetic was my pupil,
Who received all his art from me alone,
By the grace of God;
I complete my course on the journey
In three hundred fifty and eight days; <note>This duration likely refers to a symbolic alchemical or ritual year rather than Mercury's actual orbital period (approx. 88 days).</note>
And if the world were even so wide,
Prepared with many arts,
No man shall make the great Stone <term>Stone: The Philosopher's Stone (*Lapis Philosophorum*), the legendary goal of alchemy said to transform base metals into gold and provide the Elixir of Life.</term>
Unless I am also present;
Understand, whether you be lord or servant,
Pay attention, hear my words aright,
And take good heed of my meaning:
I am not common quicksilver, <note>In alchemical literature, a sharp distinction is made between "common mercury" (the metal) and "Philosophical Mercury" (the living spirit or principle of transformation).</note>
Born of a noble kind,
Before I became an eagle; <note>The "Eagle" is a common alchemical symbol for the process of sublimation, where a substance is purified by being turned into vapor.</note>
Now I have wonderful wings
On hands and feet, head and hair,
Moreover, on my entire body,
I am completely clothed in feathers.
Now, if a man were so wise,
Who with understanding and right skill,
Could manage to clip my wings, <note>Original: *abzuschießen* (to shoot off). In alchemy, "clipping the wings" refers to "fixation"—turning a volatile, evaporating substance into a stable, solid form.</note>
He would enjoy the fruits of me excellently,
And could possess more goods and gold
Than the whole world would believe.

<meta>catchword: Much</meta>

<summary>Mercury describes himself as a volatile, winged figure of "noble kind" who is the source of mathematical arts and essential to the creation of the Philosopher's Stone. He explains that his power can only be harnessed if a wise person learns to "fix" his volatile nature, symbolically described as clipping his wings.</summary>

--- Page 278 ---
<meta>This page concludes the speech of Mercury (the sixth planet) and introduces the speech of Luna (the Moon, the seventh planet). Mercury describes his role as the "messenger" who moves between the highest heavens and the depths of the "hellish" matter to unify the other planets. The transition to Luna shifts the perspective to the feminine principle of alchemy, emphasizing her role in maintaining the "lineage" or "seed" of the King (the Sun/Gold) through her cold, receptive nature.</meta>

<page-num>254</page-num>
<header>On Properties</header>

But many people mock this writing,
And will not know
What is beginning, middle, or end,
Before they experience what I accomplish.
I will now take flight,
From here to the highest throne,
And descend again to hell, <note>In alchemical philosophy, this "descent" often refers to *nigredo* (blackening), the stage where matter is broken down into its primary components.</note>
To my other six companions; <note>The "six companions" refers to the other six celestial bodies of traditional astronomy: Saturn, Jupiter, Mars, Sun, Venus, and Moon.</note>
I will see how it goes for each one,
Whether he withstands frost and heat,
Also whether they will faithfully pledge themselves to me,
So that I can perform the highest work;
And if the King <term>King: A symbol for the Sun (*Sol*), representing the perfected spirit or the final stage of the alchemical process: Gold.</term> then keeps me,
Then he remains Lord of the entire world;
This can also succeed for Rupert, <note>original: *Ruperto*. This likely refers to Prince Rupert of the Rhine (1619–1682), a famous polymath and alchemist who was a patron of such works.</note>
Therefore you may well sing the Creed. <note>original: *Credo*. To "sing the Creed" implies a state of completion, ritual success, or divine affirmation.</note>

## Of the seventh Planet
### LUNA. <note>The Moon.</note>

If I were not a feminine form,
Devoted so gently to the King,
Where then would his lineage remain,
Which I must maintain so rightly?
My cold body is wonderful,
Which the bright King warms for me;
Venus gave the enticement to this,
So that I first surrendered my will;
Gabriel <note>In Western esoteric traditions, the Archangel Gabriel is the celestial governor of the Moon.</note> brought me the announcement,
That I was betrothed to the King.

<meta>catchword: And</meta>

<summary>This page concludes Mercury's description of his volatile nature and introduces Luna (the Moon) as the feminine counterpart to the King (the Sun). It highlights the planetary relationships and the spiritual/symbolic marriage required for alchemical success.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the monologue of Luna (the Moon). Having established her feminine relationship to the King (Sol/Gold) on the previous page, she now describes the tokens of their union, her celestial cycles, and her medicinal-alchemical properties.</meta>

<page-num>255</page-num>
->of the 7th Planet.<-

And he brought me a stone so precious,
Set in gold; it was a sapphire,
Transparent, blue, adorned with color,
Given to me in honor of the King.
And he told me, by my loyalty,
That I should be the King’s wife;
To this I should resolve myself,
Into all his goods he would lead me.
My best art I employ there,
Which is called *Dialectica*; <note>original: *Dialectica*. One of the seven liberal arts, referring here to the logic or "reasoning" of the chemical transformation.</note>
And I speak as befits a woman,
For *Prudentia* instructs me. <note>original: *Prudentia*. The virtue of Prudence or Wisdom.</note>
I promised him indeed my noble body,
Until death separates us from each other,
And I have fully completed my course;
This occurs in twenty-seven days,
Seven hours, and twenty-three minutes, <note>This is a precise historical calculation of the sidereal month—the time it takes the Moon to return to the same position relative to the stars.</note>
Running through the entire circle so well.
In nineteen years upon the cycle, <note>This refers to the Metonic cycle, where the phases of the moon fall on the same days of the year every 19 years.</note>
Then I begin it all anew;
And when I calculate it round and round,
I come around every month.
My soul, my spirit, and my body,
I have dedicated to my King.
That very thing was on a Monday, <note>Monday is traditionally "Moon-day" (*Lunis dies*), the day associated with the planet Luna.</note>
As I had promised him.
To mankind I bring great power,
If I am made *potabile*; <term>Potabile: Literally "drinkable." In alchemy, "potable silver" (*Argentum Potabile*) was a medicinal elixir believed to have regenerative properties.</term>
I am blue within, white without,
When I attain the fixed prize. <term>Fixed prize: A reference to "fixation," the alchemical process of making a volatile substance stable and resistant to fire.</term>

<meta>catchword: So</meta>

<summary>Luna describes her betrothal to the King (the Sun) through the gift of a sapphire and her commitment to his service. She details her astronomical cycles (the month and the 19-year Metonic cycle) and her alchemical role as a "potable" medicine that achieves stability through fixation.</summary>

--- Page 280 ---
<meta>This page concludes the speech of Luna (the Moon/Silver). She describes her final transformation through the influence of other planetary deities and chemical substances, ending with a traditional Latin maxim on the completion of the Great Work.</meta>

<page-num>256</page-num>

Thus Venus clothes me straight away
&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;In scarlet and beautiful purple robes,
Which she indeed from Mars <note>original: *Marti*. In alchemical symbolism, Venus represents Copper and Mars represents Iron. Their "clothing" the Moon suggests the coloring of silver during the process of transmutation.</note>
&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;Obtained through pleas and great grace;
And if one were to ask Antimony, <term>Antimony: Known in German as *Spießglas*, this was a vital mineral used by alchemists to purify gold and silver.</term>
&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;Sulfur would also have something to say.
With this, I depart from here;
&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;Another may also find something;
In the end, one makes the final seal; <note>original: *gelack*. This likely refers to the "lacquer" or the final coating and perfection of the alchemical stone.</note>
&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;The outcome proves the deeds. <note>original: *Exitus Acta probat*. A Latin proverb emphasizing that the success of an experiment is only known by its final result.</note>
That has always been my rhyme,
&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;By day and also by moonlight.
Though I fly through the firmament, <note>The "firmament" refers to the celestial sphere or the heavens.</note>
&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;Everything is finally found at the end.
